500 norm beliefs from memory_llm models (experiment/results/memory_llm), sampled at random (seed 0) stratified evenly over the 4 norm families (queuing/region/trajectory/allocation) x 7 Likert levels (17-18 per cell)
from all 72232 judged per-decision beliefs of the interactive stage across 8 models.
Judge: openai/gpt-5.6-sol (blind), 7-point Likert rubric v3: 1 = unrelated/too vague, 4 = right family, rule about half right, 7 = captures the essential rule.
Score is the Likert rating rescaled to [0,1] as (likert-1)/6. Ground truth from src/norms/norm_descriptions.json.
Judge cache: experiment/plot/belief_judgements.json

====================================================================================================
ENTRY 1
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: bend_right2_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 55 (subject C6)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.9

MODEL BELIEF:
none

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (bend_right2_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (one bend, right 2): from the start cell go 2 right, then straight away from the counter for the rest of the line. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,7) → (2,8) → (3,8) → (4,8) → (5,8) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: No hypothesis is provided, so it gives an agent no guidance for following the required fixed-path queueing rule.

ENTRY 2
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: edge_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 95 (subject C3)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.95

MODEL BELIEF:
none

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (edge_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter. Unlike the other line norms, this path does not start in front of the counter: it starts at the cell right beside the counter, one step to the left (from the point of view of someone facing the counter), so the head of the line stands against the wall. From there the path runs along the wall, away from the counter, over walkable cells (skipping dead ends), and stops once it is 4 cells from the counter. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6)): the path is (1,5) → (1,4) → (1,3) → (1,2) → (2,2) → (3,2) → (3,1) → (4,1).

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The candidate provides no rule or actionable guidance about the required queue path or behavior.

ENTRY 3
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: lshape_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 49 (subject C7)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.9

MODEL BELIEF:
The social norm is that agents should not block or interfere with the counter area, and agents must yield to others when approaching the counter.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (lshape_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (L, left): from the start cell go 2 away, then 2 left, then straight away from the counter for the rest of the line. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (3,6) → (4,6) → (4,5) → (4,4) → (5,4) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: It addresses conduct near the counter and yielding to others, but omits the required fixed L-shaped queue path, joining at its last free cell, and advancing only when the next cell is empty.

ENTRY 4
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: occupation_seat_bottom_left_only  (family: region)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 86 (subject C7)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.8

MODEL BELIEF:
none

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (occupation_seat_bottom_left_only):
Agents may only sit in the bottom-left region of the room. That region is the corner square enclosed by the left door and the bottom door: every cell in the rows from the left door's row down to the bottom wall and in the columns from the left wall right to the bottom door's column. All agents, regardless of class, must choose a chair inside that bottom-left region as their goal seat and may not occupy any chair outside it.

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The candidate provides no rule, so it cannot guide agents to choose seats only within the bottom-left region.

MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: occupation_seat_no_bottom_right  (family: region)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 27 (subject C6)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.85

MODEL BELIEF:
Agents are following their own goals without conflict, but there may be an unspoken norm to avoid blocking pathways or entrances.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (occupation_seat_no_bottom_right):
Agents may not sit in the bottom-right region of the room. That region is the corner square enclosed by the bottom door and the right wall: every cell in the rows from the left door's row down to the bottom wall and in the columns to the right of the bottom door's column. All agents, regardless of class, must choose a chair outside that bottom-right region as their goal seat and may not occupy any chair inside it.

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis notices avoidance around entrances or pathways but does not identify the prohibited bottom-right seating region and would not reliably prevent agents from choosing chairs there.

ENTRY 10
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: square_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 53 (subject C5)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.95

MODEL BELIEF:
No social norm observed yet.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (square_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (square, left): a fixed loop of exactly 7 cells — from the start cell go 1 left, 2 away, 2 right, then 1 toward the counter, ending one cell right-and-away of the start. The line never extends beyond these 7 cells. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (4,5) → (4,6) → (4,7) → (3,7).

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis states no behavioral rule and therefore gives an agent no guidance about the required fixed seven-cell queue path or orderly advancement.

ENTRY 11
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct
NORM: zigzag_left2_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 73 (subject C4)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.9

MODEL BELIEF:
Agents are expected to avoid collisions and move in a coordinated manner; violations occur when agents push or move into occupied spaces without coordination.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (zigzag_left2_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (zigzag, left first, 2 wide): from the start cell go 2 left, then 1 away, then 2 right, then 1 away, then 2 left, and so on — a snake that always moves away from the counter. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (2,4) → (3,4) → (3,5) → (3,6) → (4,6) → (4,5) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis addresses occupied-space coordination but misses the fixed zigzag queue path, joining at its last free cell, orderly advancement, and prohibition on cutting or waiting off-path.

ENTRY 19
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct
NORM: movement_move1_wait1  (family: trajectory)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 14 (subject C3)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.5

MODEL BELIEF:
Ash's red cross indicates a violation of a social norm, possibly related to blocking or occupying a position that is not allowed. (confidence: 0.50)

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (movement_move1_wait1):
Agents must never move on two consecutive turns. After every turn on which an agent moves, it must stay stationary for the next turn before it may move again (move, wait, move, wait, …). This rhythm is counted on each agent's own moves, not on the global turn number: an agent may always wait longer than required, and only a turn on which the agent actually moved counts as a move.

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis does not identify the required move-then-wait rhythm and instead speculates about blocking or forbidden positions, which is unrelated to the norm.

Agents may not move on any turn whose number leaves a remainder of 2 when divided by 3 (turn 2, 5, 8, 11, …). The turn number is the global step counter of the environment, shared by every agent.

ENTRY 29
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct
NORM: occupation_seat_in_order  (family: allocation)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 89 (subject C7)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.82

MODEL BELIEF:
Avoid moving into a space occupied by another agent or blocking their path.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (occupation_seat_in_order):
Agents must occupy the seats in a fixed order, filling them column by column: starting with the leftmost column, fill its chairs from top to bottom, then move to the next column to the right and fill it from top to bottom, and so on. The first seat is the topmost chair in the leftmost column; once a column is fully filled, the next seat is the topmost chair in the column immediately to its right. Seats are claimed sequentially: each arriving agent must take the seat that comes immediately after the last seat that was filled, never an earlier one. This ordering only ever moves forward — if an agent sitting in an earlier seat finishes and leaves, that now-empty seat is not reused; agents continue filling later seats in order. Only once the very last seat in the sequence has been filled does the order wrap back around and start over from the first seat.

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis is unrelated to the required sequential seat-occupation order and gives no guidance on which seat an arriving agent should take.

ENTRY 32
MODEL: Qwen_Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct
NORM: square_right_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 118 (subject C11)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.0

MODEL BELIEF:
none

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (square_right_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (square, right): a fixed loop of exactly 7 cells — from the start cell go 1 right, 2 away, 2 left, then 1 toward the counter, ending one cell left-and-away of the start. The line never extends beyond these 7 cells. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,7) → (3,7) → (4,7) → (4,6) → (4,5) → (3,5).

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The candidate provides no rule or actionable guidance about the required queue path or movement behavior.

====================================================================================================

Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (2,4) → (3,4) → (3,5) → (3,6) → (4,6) → (4,5) → ...

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (bend_left2_line):

The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (one bend, left 2): from the start cell go 2 left, then straight away from the counter for the rest of the line. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (2,4) → (3,4) → (4,4) → (5,4) → ...

Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6)): the path is (1,7) → (1,8) → (1,9) → (1,10) → (1,11) → (2,11) → (3,11) → (4,11) → (4,12) → (4,13).

JUDGE LIKERT: 4/7  (score 0.5)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly requires a single queue, joining at the back, and advancing one cell at a time, but places the queue on the wrong fixed path and adds unsupported exit constraints.

MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.5

MODEL BELIEF:

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (movement_clockwise_move):
Agents must circulate through the environment in a clockwise direction, and they must do so along the walls: all movement has to stay on the outermost ring of walkable cells that hugs the edge of the map (the cells right next to the surrounding walls), never cutting through the interior of the space. Following this ring clockwise means moving right along the top wall, down along the right wall, left along the bottom wall, and up along the left wall. Each move must be to the next cell along the ring in that direction; agents must not move counterclockwise, and must not leave the ring except to step onto it from a door or to take the final step onto their goal cell.

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis addresses directional movement and correctly favors downward travel in some locations, but replaces the clockwise perimeter rule with queueing and shortest-route constraints and wrongly prohibits westward movement, which is required along the bottom.

Following this ring counterclockwise means moving left along the top wall, down along the left wall, right along the bottom wall, and up along the right wall. Each move must be to the next cell along the ring in that direction; agents must not move clockwise, and must not leave the ring except to step onto it from a door or to take the final step onto their goal cell.

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis partially recognizes that agents should use designated outer walkways, but it entirely omits the essential counterclockwise direction and adds unrelated collision, queueing, and penalty rules.

Agents must occupy the seats in a fixed order, filling them column by column: starting with the leftmost column, fill its chairs from top to bottom, then move to the next column to the right and fill it from top to bottom, and so on. The first seat is the topmost chair in the leftmost column; once a column is fully filled, the next seat is the topmost chair in the column immediately to its right. Seats are claimed sequentially: each arriving agent must take the seat that comes immediately after the last seat that was filled, never an earlier one. This ordering only ever moves forward — if an agent sitting in an earlier seat finishes and leaves, that now-empty seat is not reused; agents continue filling later seats in order. Only once the very last seat in the sequence has been filled does the order wrap back around and start over from the first seat.

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis addresses seat selection but replaces the required column-by-column sequential order with an incorrect zone-segregation rule and does not capture forward progression or wraparound.

ENTRY 151
MODEL: anthropic_claude-opus-5
NORM: square_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 68 (subject C11)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.85

MODEL BELIEF:
Ordering requires a strict single-file queue feeding the counter cell (2,6); the line extends away from the counter (currently downward in column 5: (2,5),(3,5),(4,5),(5,5)...). Newcomers must approach from behind via the open lower rows and attach at the tail, then advance one cell at a time into the empty space directly ahead, keeping the line compact. Never overtake, cut in beside/ahead of queued agents, push the agent at the counter, or loiter in the side corridor next to the line (e.g. (3,4)). Only the front-of-line agent enters (2,6), and only after the previous customer is served and leaves. Once served (checkmark), vacate promptly and exit via the open row-5 corridor, avoiding the queue lane and restricted zone R (rows 1-3, cols 10-11).

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (square_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (square, left): a fixed loop of exactly 7 cells — from the start cell go 1 left, 2 away, 2 right, then 1 toward the counter, ending one cell right-and-away of the start. The line never extends beyond these 7 cells. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (4,5) → (4,6) → (4,7) → (3,7).

JUDGE LIKERT: 5/7  (score 0.667)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly specifies strict single-file tail joining, no cutting, and one-cell advancement into vacated space, but substantially misstates the fixed seven-cell square-left path by extending it straight downward instead of following its turns and endpoint.

ENTRY 183
MODEL: anthropic_claude-opus-5
NORM: zigzag_left2_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 178 (subject C14)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.89

MODEL BELIEF:
Counter-queue etiquette with strict priority: a single line forms along row 2 heading east to the service cell (2,6); join at the western back of the line ((2,2), since (2,1) is a table), advance only into the adjacent free slot, never cut in from a side lane, from the east, or via staff row 1, and never push a queued or being-served agent. Idling on the open floor when a legitimate advance exists is penalised, but waiting is acceptable when the cell ahead is legitimately occupied or while standing on your own goal cell. After service, vacate the counter promptly and exit through open corridors (row 5, column 7/zone Q), avoiding zone R when merely transiting and never pushing someone standing in a doorway.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (zigzag_left2_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (zigzag, left first, 2 wide): from the start cell go 2 left, then 1 away, then 2 right, then 1 away, then 2 left, and so on — a snake that always moves away from the counter. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (2,4) → (3,4) → (3,5) → (3,6) → (4,6) → (4,5) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 4/7  (score 0.5)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly requires joining the back, advancing one adjacent slot at a time, and not cutting, but it replaces the required fixed two-wide zigzag path with an incorrect straight row-2 line and adds unrelated post-service constraints.

====================================================================================================

Shape (one bend, left 1): from the start cell go 1 left, then straight away from the counter for the rest of the line. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (4,5) → (5,5) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: It addresses where agents may wait near the counter, but incorrectly bans row 2 rather than requiring agents to occupy and advance along the fixed bent queue path, which includes row-2 cells.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (movement_clockwise_move):

This ordering only ever moves forward — if an agent sitting in an earlier seat finishes and leaves, that now-empty seat is not reused; agents continue filling later seats in order. Only once the very last seat in the sequence has been filled does the order wrap back around and start over from the first seat.

ENTRY 261
MODEL: google_gemini-3.1-pro-preview
NORM: square_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 159 (subject C5)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.95

MODEL BELIEF:
Agents must wait in the designated queue area (Zone R) when the counter is occupied, and can proceed only when the counter target (2, 6) is completely empty.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (square_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (square, left): a fixed loop of exactly 7 cells — from the start cell go 1 left, 2 away, 2 right, then 1 toward the counter, ending one cell right-and-away of the start. The line never extends beyond these 7 cells. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (4,5) → (4,6) → (4,7) → (3,7).

JUDGE LIKERT: 3/7  (score 0.333)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly identifies designated queueing and waiting for an empty target, but omits the fixed seven-cell path, joining at its last free cell, and advancing one position only when the cell immediately ahead is empty.

The line never extends beyond these 7 cells. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,7) → (3,7) → (4,7) → (4,6) → (4,5) → (3,5).

JUDGE LIKERT: 3/7  (score 0.333)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly identifies ordered queueing and forbids cutting, but replaces the required seven-cell square-right path with an incorrect vertical queue in one column.

Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (3,4) → (4,4) → (5,4) → ...

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (zigzag_left1_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (zigzag, left first, 1 wide): from the start cell go 1 left, then 1 away, then 1 right, then 1 away, then 1 left, and so on — a tight zigzag that always moves away from the counter. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (3,6) → (4,6) → (4,5) → (5,5) → (5,6) → ...

JUDGE REASON: It correctly identifies a fixed queue beginning at (2,6) and initially extending left, but incorrectly makes the entire line horizontal instead of the required tight zigzag and adds an unsupported exit rule.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (edge_right_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter. Unlike the other line norms, this path does not start in front of the counter: it starts at the cell right beside the counter, one step to the right (from the point of view of someone facing the counter), so the head of the line stands against the wall. From there the path runs along the wall, away from the counter, over walkable cells (skipping dead ends), and stops once it is 4 cells from the counter. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6)): the path is (1,7) → (1,8) → (1,9) → (1,10) → (1,11) → (2,11) → (3,11) → (4,11) → (4,12) → (4,13).

JUDGE LIKERT: 3/7  (score 0.333)
JUDGE REASON: It captures the no-cutting, arrival-order aspect of the norm but omits the essential fixed-path placement and one-cell-at-a-time movement rules.

NORM: movement_move3_wait1  (family: trajectory)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 166 (subject C5)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.9

MODEL BELIEF:
avoiding collisions with other agents

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (movement_move3_wait1):
Agents must never move on four consecutive turns. After moving on three turns in a row, an agent must stay stationary for the next turn before it may move again (move, move, move, wait, move, move, move, wait, …). This rhythm is counted on each agent's own moves, not on the global turn number: an agent may always wait longer than required, and only a turn on which the agent actually moved counts as a move.

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The hypothesis is unrelated to the required individual move-three-then-wait rhythm and would not prevent four consecutive moves.

ENTRY 305

NORM: square_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 64 (subject C5)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.95

MODEL BELIEF:
The agents should wait for the previous agent to finish their task before moving to the next one.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (square_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (square, left): a fixed loop of exactly 7 cells — from the start cell go 1 left, 2 away, 2 right, then 1 toward the counter, ending one cell right-and-away of the start. The line never extends beyond these 7 cells. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (3,5) → (4,5) → (4,6) → (4,7) → (3,7).

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: It vaguely captures waiting for the agent ahead, but omits the fixed seven-cell path, rear joining, one-cell advancement, and the requirement to move only when the next cell is empty.

With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (one bend, left 2): from the start cell go 2 left, then straight away from the counter for the rest of the line. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,5) → (2,4) → (3,4) → (4,4) → (5,4) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 4/7  (score 0.5)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly captures joining at the back, preserving order, and advancing only when space opens, but omits the essential fixed bent path and its mandatory use even when no queue is present.

Customers must use and follow the designated queue area before proceeding to their chair; bypassing the queue or using marked route zones as shortcuts violates the norm.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (movement_counterclockwise_move):
Agents must circulate through the environment in a counterclockwise direction, and they must do so along the walls: all movement has to stay on the outermost ring of walkable cells that hugs the edge of the map (the cells right next to the surrounding walls), never cutting through the interior of the space. Following this ring counterclockwise means moving left along the top wall, down along the left wall, right along the bottom wall, and up along the right wall. Each move must be to the next cell along the ring in that direction; agents must not move clockwise, and must not leave the ring except to step onto it from a door or to take the final step onto their goal cell.

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: It weakly captures the prohibition on shortcuts by requiring a designated route, but incorrectly frames the norm as queue use and omits the essential outer-wall ring and counterclockwise movement requirements.

Agents must never move on two consecutive turns. After every turn on which an agent moves, it must stay stationary for the next turn before it may move again (move, wait, move, wait, …). ENTRY 466
MODEL: openai_gpt-5.6-sol
NORM: staircase_right_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 89 (subject C6)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.99

MODEL BELIEF:
Customers must yield while another customer is being served by remaining outside the counter's immediate waiting and approach area; contesting, blocking, or loitering near an occupied counter is prohibited.

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (staircase_right_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). The path starts at the cell directly in front of the counter (below it when the counter is on the top wall; beside it when the counter is on a side wall). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter: 'away' = one step further from the counter, 'right'/'left' = that person's right/left. With the counter on the top wall: away = down, right = screen-right, left = screen-left. Shape (staircase, right): from the start cell go 1 right, 1 away, 1 right, 1 away (two stair steps), then straight away from the counter for the rest of the line. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,7) → (3,7) → (3,8) → (4,8) → (5,8) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 2/7  (score 0.167)
JUDGE REASON: It addresses waiting and yielding at an occupied counter, but replaces the required fixed staircase queue path and orderly advancement with a largely incompatible rule to stay away from the counter area.

Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6), start cell (2,6)): the path is (2,6) → (2,7) → (2,8) → (3,8) → (3,7) → (3,6) → (4,6) → (4,7) → ...

JUDGE LIKERT: 4/7  (score 0.5)
JUDGE REASON: It correctly captures joining at the back, advancing only into an empty position, and avoiding cutting, but omits the mandatory fixed zigzag path and its use even when no queue is present.

ENTRY 484
MODEL: zai-org_GLM-4.6V-Flash
NORM: edge_left_line  (family: queuing)
INTERACTIVE STEP: 43 (subject C3)
MODEL CONFIDENCE: 0.0

MODEL BELIEF:
none

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (edge_left_line):
Agents waiting for the counter must line up along a fixed path of cells. This path must be followed whether or not anyone else is already in line: an agent approaching the counter walks to the last free cell of the path, then advances one cell at a time only after the agent ahead has moved forward, until it reaches the head of the path and is served. An agent may only move forward one position in the line once the cell ahead is completely empty. Stepping off the path, waiting near the counter on a cell that is not on the path, or cutting ahead is a violation (the only exception is when every path cell is already occupied). Directions are from the point of view of someone standing in line facing the counter. Unlike the other line norms, this path does not start in front of the counter: it starts at the cell right beside the counter, one step to the left (from the point of view of someone facing the counter), so the head of the line stands against the wall. From there the path runs along the wall, away from the counter, over walkable cells (skipping dead ends), and stops once it is 4 cells from the counter. Example, in the cafe (counter at (1,6)): the path is (1,5) → (1,4) → (1,3) → (1,2) → (2,2) → (3,2) → (3,1) → (4,1).

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The candidate provides no rule or actionable guidance about the required queue path or behavior.

MODEL BELIEF:
none

GROUND-TRUTH NORM (movement_move3_wait1):
Agents must never move on four consecutive turns. After moving on three turns in a row, an agent must stay stationary for the next turn before it may move again (move, move, move, wait, move, move, move, wait, …). This rhythm is counted on each agent's own moves, not on the global turn number: an agent may always wait longer than required, and only a turn on which the agent actually moved counts as a move.

JUDGE LIKERT: 1/7  (score 0.0)
JUDGE REASON: The candidate provides no behavioral rule, so it cannot guide an agent to follow the required move-three-then-wait rhythm.
